p1 c0 15.9pt | **Aspergillus** **section** **Nidulantes** **(formerly** **Emericella****):** **Polyphasic**
p1 c0 15.9pt | **taxonomy,** **chemistry** **and** **biology**
p1 c0 10.2pt | A.J. Chen1,2, J.C. Frisvad3*, B.D. Sun7, J. Varga4, S. Kocsub�e4, J. Dijksterhuis2, D.H. Kim5, S.-B. Hong6, J. Houbraken2, and
p1 c0 10.2pt | R.A. Samson2*
p1 c0  8.1pt | **Abstract:** _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ includes species with striking morphological characters, such as biseriate conidiophores with brown-pigmented stipes, and if
p1 c0  8.1pt | present, the production of ascomata embedded in masses of Hülle cells with often reddish brown ascospores. The majority of species in this section have a sexual state,
p1 c0  8.1pt | which were named _Emericella_ in the dual name nomenclature system. In the present study, strains belonging to subgenus _Nidulantes_ were subjected to multilocus
p1 c0  8.1pt | molecular phylogenetic analyses using internal transcribed spacer region (ITS), partial β-tubulin (_BenA_), calmodulin (_CaM_) and RNA polymerase II second largest subunit
p1 c0  8.1pt | (_RPB2_) sequences. Nine sections are accepted in subgenus_ Nidulantes_ including the new section_ Cavernicolus._ A polyphasic approach using morphological characters,
p1 c0  8.1pt | extrolites, physiological characters and phylogeny was applied to investigate the taxonomy of section _Nidulantes_. Based on this approach, section _Nidulantes_ is
p1 c0  8.1pt | subdivided in seven clades and 65 species, and 10 species are described here as new. Morphological characters including colour, shape, size, and ornamentation of
p1 c0  8.1pt | ascospores, shape and size of conidia and vesicles, growth temperatures are important for identifying species. Many species of section _Nidulantes_ produce the
p1 c0  8.1pt | carcinogenic mycotoxin sterigmatocystin. The most important mycotoxins in _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ are aflatoxins, sterigmatocystin, emestrin, fumitremorgins,
p1 c0  8.1pt | asteltoxins, and paxillin while other extrolites are useful drugs or drug lead candidates such as echinocandins, mulundocandins, calbistrins, varitriols, variecolins and
p1 c0  8.1pt | terrain. Aflatoxin B1 is produced by four species: _A._ _astellatus,_ _A._ _miraensis,_ _A._ _olivicola_, and _A._ _venezuelensis._
p1 c0  8.1pt | **Key** **words:** _Ascomycetes_, _Eurotiales_, Multi-gene phylogeny, Sterigmatocystin.
p1 c0  8.1pt | **Taxonomic** **novelties:** **New** **section:** Section _Cavernicolus_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson; **New** **species:** _Aspergillus_ _angustatus_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson,
p1 c0  8.1pt | _A._ _aurantiopurpureus_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _botswanensis_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _dromiae_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _israelensis_ A.J.
p1 c0  8.1pt | Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _latilabiatus_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _savannensis_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _stercorarius_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad &
p1 c0  8.1pt | Samson, _A._ _sulphureoviridis_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson, _A._ _viridicatenatus_ A.J. Chen, Frisvad & Samson.
p1 c0 10.0pt | Available online 19 October 2016; http://dx.doi.org/10.1016/j.simyco.2016.10.001.
p1 c0 12.2pt | **INTRODUCTION**
p1 c0 10.2pt | The species of _Aspergillus_ fall into distinct clusters, which have
p1 c0 10.2pt | been called “groups” by Thom & Church (1926), Thom & Raper
p1 c0 10.2pt | (1945) and Raper & Fennell (1965). These groups do not have
p1 c0 10.2pt | nomenclatural standing and therefore Gams _et_ _al._ (1985) intro-
p1 c0 10.2pt | duced formal names for these groups as subgenera and sec-
p1 c0 10.2pt | tions. Subgenus _Nidulantes_ contained five sections, namely
p1 c0 10.2pt | sections _Nidulantes_, _Versicolores_, _Usti_, _Terrei_, and _Flavipedes_.
p1 c0 10.2pt | Several investigations were conducted for nearly 20 years to test
p1 c0 10.2pt | the taxonomic hypotheses based on phenotypic analysis.
p1 c0 10.2pt | Peterson (2008) and Peterson _et_ _al._ (2008) assessed phyloge-
p1 c0 10.2pt | netic relationships across _Aspergillus_ using four loci and they
p1 c0 10.2pt | accepted sections _Nidulantes_, _Usti_, _Ochraceorosei_, _Sparsi_ and
p1 c0 10.2pt | three hypothetical sections _Raperi_, _Silvati_, _Bispori_. Varga _et_ _al._
p1 c0 10.2pt | (2010a, 2010b) introduced sections _Aenei_ and _Sparsi_ based
p1 c0 10.2pt | on _CaM_, _BenA_ and ITS sequence data, whereas Houbraken
p1 c0 10.2pt | _et_ _al._ (2014) accepted eight sections namely _Aenei_, _Bispori_,
p1 c0 10.2pt | _Cremei_, _Nidulantes_, _Ochraceorosei_, _Silvati_, _Sparsi_ and _Usti_ in
p1 c0 10.2pt | subgenus_ Nidulantes_. Until now approximately 100 species have
p1 c0 10.2pt | been described in this subgenus. The indoor relevant species in
p1 c0 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ subgenus_ Nidulantes_ section_ Versicolores_ are closely

=== page 1, right column ===
p1 c1 10.2pt | related to species in section_ Nidulantes_ (Raper & Fennell 1965,
p1 c1 10.2pt | Klich 1993, Jurjevic _et_ _al._ 2012).
p1 c1 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ accommodates _Aspergillus_
p1 c1 10.2pt | _nidulans_ and other species producing biseriate conidiophores
p1 c1 10.2pt | with pale brown pigmented stipes, and if present, the ascomata
p1 c1 10.2pt | embedded in masses of Hülle cells (Frisvad & Samson 2004,
p1 c1 10.2pt | Horie 1978, 1979, 1980, Kong & Qi 1986, Horie _et_ _al._ 1989,
p1 c1 10.2pt | 1990, 1996a,b, 1998, 2000, Raper & Fennell 1965, Samson &
p1 c1 10.2pt | Mouchacca 1975, Stchigel & Guarro 1997, Thom & Raper
p1 c1 10.2pt | 1939, Zalar _et_ _al._ 2008 and others). The majority of section
p1 c1 10.2pt | _Nidulantes_ species are able to produce a sexual state and those
p1 c1 10.2pt | species were, in the dual name nomenclature system, assigned
p1 c1 10.2pt | to the genus _Emericella._ Because of the adoption of the “one
p1 c1 10.2pt | fungus: one name” nomenclatural system, all_ Emericella_ species
p1 c1 10.2pt | have been transferred to_ Aspergillus_ (Samson_ et al._ 2014). Most
p1 c1 10.2pt | former _Emericella_ species belong to _Aspergillus_ subgenus
p1 c1 10.2pt | _Nidulantes_ section _Nidulantes_. The only exceptions are: 1)
p1 c1 10.2pt | _Aspergillus heterothallicus_ (=_ Emericella heterothallica_), the only
p1 c1 10.2pt | known heterothallic species in subgenus _Nidulantes_, currently
p1 c1 10.2pt | classified in _Aspergillus_ subgenus _Nidulantes_ section _Usti_
p1 c1 10.2pt | (Houbraken _et_ _al._ 2007, Samson _et_ _al._ 2011), and 2) _A._ _bicolor_
p1 c1 10.2pt | (=_E._ _bicolor_)_,_ _A._ _discophorus_ (_=E._ _discophora_)_,_ _A._ _foeniculicola_

=== page 2, left column ===
p2 c0 10.2pt | (_=E. foeniculicola_), and_ A. spectabilis_ (_=E. spectabilis_) classified
p2 c0 10.2pt | in _Aspergillus_ subgenus _Nidulantes_ section _Aenei_ (Varga _et_ _al._
p2 c0 10.2pt | 2010a).
p2 c0 10.2pt | The morphology of the ascospores including colour, shape,
p2 c0 10.2pt | size and ornamentation are of particular importance for species
p2 c0 10.2pt | delineation and identification in_ Emericella_ (Thom & Raper 1939,
p2 c0 10.2pt | Christensen & Raper 1978, Horie 1980, Christensen & States
p2 c0 10.2pt | 1982, Ismail _et_ _al._ 1995, Zalar _et_ _al._ 2008, Matsuzawa _et_ _al._
p2 c0 10.2pt | 2012, Guarro _et_ _al._ 2012, Kritmitzas _et_ _al._ 2013). Nowadays
p2 c0 10.2pt | multiple methods are applied for species recognition and for
p2 c0 10.2pt | example Frisvad & Samson (2004) applied a polyphasic analysis
p2 c0 10.2pt | and described _A._ _venezuelensis_ (= _E._ _venezuelensis_) based on
p2 c0 10.2pt | morphological characters, extrolites and phylogenetic analyses.
p2 c0 10.2pt | Using molecular phylogenetics, morphological data and growth
p2 c0 10.2pt | temperatures Matsuzawa _et_ _al._ (2012) discussed the species
p2 c0 10.2pt | concept in _Emericella_ and found that several species including
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _nidulans_ (= _E._ _nidulans_), _A._ _dentatus_ (= _E._ _dentata_),
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _sublatus_ (= _E._ _sublata_), _A._ _montenegroi_ (= _E._ _montenegroi_),
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A. nidulans_ var._ latus_ (=_ E. nidulans_ var._ lata_),_ A. quadrilineatus_ (=
p2 c0 10.2pt | _E._ _quadrilineata_), _A._ _miyajii_ (= _E._ _miyajii_), _A._ _parvathecius_ (=
p2 c0 10.2pt | _E._ _parvathecia_) and _A._ _acristatus_ (= _E._ _acristata_) were undis-
p2 c0 10.2pt | tinguishable by phylogenetic analysis alone. Therefore, they
p2 c0 10.2pt | suggested to evaluate phylogenetic, morphological and physio-
p2 c0 10.2pt | logical characters to identify species in this genus or section.
p2 c0 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ species are widely distributed
p2 c0 10.2pt | in nature and are believed to play significant roles in decom-
p2 c0 10.2pt | position processes (Raper & Fennell 1965). The most well-
p2 c0 10.2pt | known species _A._ _nidulans_, with the whole genome being
p2 c0 10.2pt | sequenced in 2005 (Galaghan _et al._ 2005), occupies a place of
p2 c0 10.2pt | prominence second only to _Neurospora_ in the field of fungal
p2 c0 10.2pt | genetics, being used to study a wide range of subjects including
p2 c0 10.2pt | recombination, DNA repair, mutation, cell cycle control, nucleo-
p2 c0 10.2pt | kinesis, pathogenesis, metabolism, and experimental evolution
p2 c0 10.2pt | (Pontecorvo_ et al._ 1954, Herbert & Arst 1976, Dean & Timberlake
p2 c0 10.2pt | 1989, Schoustra_ et al._ 2006, Todd_ et al._ 2007). In addition to its
p2 c0 10.2pt | role as genetic model, _A._ _nidulans_ has been demonstrated as
p2 c0 10.2pt | causative agent of diverse infections in humans. It was identified
p2 c0 10.2pt | in cases of otomycosis, mycetoma, keratitis, sinusitis and pul-
p2 c0 10.2pt | monary aspergilloma and was recognised as a major cause of
p2 c0 10.2pt | invasive aspergillosis (IA) in patients with chronic granulomatous
p2 c0 10.2pt | disease (CGD) (Baylet_ et al._ 1968, Doby & Kombila-Favry 1978,
p2 c0 10.2pt | Joshi _et_ _al._ 1985, Segal _et_ _al._ 1998, Henriet _et_ _al._ 2012). Other
p2 c0 10.2pt | species in section _Nidulantes_ and _Versicolores_ such as
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _delacroxii_ (=_A._ _spinulosporus_), _A._ _dentatus_, _A._ _protuberus_,
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A. quadrilineatus, A. sublatus_,_ A. unguis_,_ A. sydowii_,_ A. stellatus_,
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _versicolor_ and _A._ _hongkongensis_ have also been reported in
p2 c0 10.2pt | human infections (Polacheck _et_ _al._ 1992, de Hoog _et_ _al._ 2000,
p2 c0 10.2pt | Verweij _et_ _al._ 2008, Arabatzis _et_ _al._ 2011, Yu _et_ _al._ 2013, de
p2 c0 10.2pt | Fontbrune _et_ _al._ 2014, Sabino _et_ _al._ 2014, Tsang _et_ _al._ 2016).
p2 c0 10.2pt | Members of _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ produce a high
p2 c0 10.2pt | number of secondary metabolites: such as aflatoxins and ster-
p2 c0 10.2pt | igmatocystins, echinocandins and mulundocandins, penicillins,
p2 c0 10.2pt | terreins, and many others (Turner 1971, Cole & Cox 1981, Turner
p2 c0 10.2pt | & Aldridge 1983, Frisvad 1985, Liu & Shen 2011, Saito _et_ _al._
p2 c0 10.2pt | 2016). In general, similar metabolites can occur in phylogeneti-
p2 c0 10.2pt | cally closely related species, for example _A._ _variecolor_ (=
p2 c0 10.2pt | _E._ _variecolor_), _A._ _fi__lifer_ (= _E._ _fi__lifera_), _A._ _stella-maris_ (= _E._ _stella-_
p2 c0 10.2pt | _maris_), _A._ _olivicola_ (= _E._ _olivicola_), _A._ _venezuelensis_ (=
p2 c0 10.2pt | _E. venezuelensis_) and_ A. astellatus (= E. astellata_) all produce the
p2 c0 10.2pt | octaketides shamixanthones, emericellin and arugosins, while
p2 c0 10.2pt | _A. pluriseminatus_ (_= E. pluriseminata_), a phylogenetically species
p2 c0 10.2pt | distant from these, showed an entirely distinctive extrolite profile

p2 c1 10.2pt | (Zalar _et_ _al._ 2008). Anidulafungin, a semisynthetic lipopeptide
p2 c1 10.2pt | antifungal drug of the echinocandin type, is derived from a
p2 c1 10.2pt | fermentation product of _A._ _spinulosporus_ (syn. _A._ _nidulans_ var.
p2 c1 10.2pt | _echinulatus_) (Nyfeler & Keller-Schierlein 1974), _A._ _parvathecius,_
p2 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _navahoensis_, _A._ _quadrilineatus_, _A._ _rugulosus_ and
p2 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _pachycristatus_ (= “_Aspergillus_ _nidulans_ var. _roseus_” _nomen_
p2 c1 10.2pt | _nudum_) (Boeckner & Kastner 1981, Klich_ et al._ 2001, de la Cruz
p2 c1 10.2pt | _et_ _al._ 2012, Matsuzawa _et_ _al._ 2012, Bills _et_ _al._ 2014, Yue _et_ _al._
p2 c1 10.2pt | 2015). Aflatoxin production is observed in _A._ _astellatus_ (_=_ _E._
p2 c1 10.2pt | _astellata_),_ A. venezuelensis_ (=_ E. venezuelensis_) and_ A. olivicola_
p2 c1 10.2pt | (=_ E. olivicola_) (Frisvad & Samson 2004, Frisvad_ et al._ 2004, Zalar
p2 c1 10.2pt | _et_ _al._ 2008). Recently, a fungal natural product aspergillomar-
p2 c1 10.2pt | asmine A (AMA) was identified from extracts of _A._ _versicolor_
p2 c1 10.2pt | (strain WAC-138). This compound combined with a carbapenem
p2 c1 10.2pt | antibiotic has therapeutic potential to address the clinical chal-
p2 c1 10.2pt | lenge of MBL (metallo-β-lactamase)-positive carbapenem-
p2 c1 10.2pt | resistant Gram-negative pathogens (King _et al._ 2014).
p2 c1 10.2pt | In this study, we delineate the sections of _Aspergillus_ sub-
p2 c1 10.2pt | genus _Nidulantes_ using a phylogenetic analysis of a combined
p2 c1 10.2pt | data set of partial ITS, β-tubulin (_BenA_), calmodulin (_CaM_) and
p2 c1 10.2pt | RNA polymerase II second largest subunit (_RPB2_) gene se-
p2 c1 10.2pt | quences. Subsequently, the taxonomy of section_ Nidulantes_ was
p2 c1 10.2pt | investigated using a polyphasic approach including sequence
p2 c1 10.2pt | analyses, morphological and physiological characterisation, and
p2 c1 10.2pt | extrolite profiles.
p2 c1 12.2pt | **MATERIAL** **AND** **METHODS**
p2 c1 12.2pt | **Fungal** **strains**
p2 c1 10.2pt | Isolates used in this study were obtained from: 1) CBS, culture
p2 c1 10.2pt | collection of CBS-KNAW Fungal Biodiversity Centre, Utrecht,
p2 c1 10.2pt | The Netherlands; 2) IBT, culture collection of the DTU Systems
p2 c1 10.2pt | Biology, Lyngby, Denmark; and 3) CGMCC, China General
p2 c1 10.2pt | Microbiological Culture Collection Centre, Beijing, China). Iso-
p2 c1 10.2pt | lates deposited in the working collection of the Applied and In-
p2 c1 10.2pt | dustrial Mycology department (DTO) housed at CBS-KNAW
p2 c1 10.2pt | were also included in this study. An overview of strains is listed in
p2 c1 10.2pt | Table 1.
p2 c1 12.2pt | **DNA** **extraction,** **PCR** **ampli****fi****cation** **and**
p2 c1 12.2pt | **sequencing**
p2 c1 10.2pt | Strains were grown for 1 wk on MEA prior to DNA extraction.
p2 c1 10.2pt | DNA was extracted using the UltracleanTM Microbial DNA
p2 c1 10.2pt | isolation Kit (MoBio, Solana Beach, U.S.A.) and stored
p2 c1 10.2pt | at −20 °C. ITS, _BenA_, _CaM_, and _RPB2_ were amplified and
p2 c1 10.2pt | sequenced using methods and primers as previously described
p2 c1 10.2pt | (Houbraken & Samson 2011, Samson _et_ _al._ 2014).
p2 c1 12.2pt | **Phylogenetic** **analysis**
p2 c1 10.2pt | The phylogenetic relationship between species was studied
p2 c1 10.2pt | using a combined data set containing ITS,_ BenA_,_ CaM_ and_ RPB2_
p2 c1 10.2pt | sequences, individual single gene phylogenies were also
p2 c1 10.2pt | generated to resolve relationships among the species. Sequence
p2 c1 10.2pt | alignments were generated with MAFFT v. 7 (Katoh & Standley
p2 c1 10.2pt | 2013). The most suitable substitution model was determined
p2 c1 10.2pt | using FindModel (Posada & Crandall 1998). Bayesian analyses

p11 c0 10.2pt | 2003). The sample frequency was set to 100 and the first 25 % of
p11 c0 10.2pt | the trees removed as burn-in. Maximum likelihood analyses
p11 c0 10.2pt | including 500 bootstrap replicates were run using RAxML
p11 c0 10.2pt | (Gamma model of rate heterogeneity) (Stamatakis _et_ _al._ 2008).
p11 c0 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ _fl__avipes_ (NRRL 302T) was used as outgroup in the
p11 c0 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ subgenus _Nidulantes_ phylogeny and _Aspergillus_

p11 c0 10.2pt | phylogeny. The resulting trees were visualized with FigTree
p11 c0 10.2pt | v1.4.2 and annotated using Adobe Illustrator CS5. BI posterior
p11 c0 10.2pt | probabilities (pp) values and bootstrap (bs) percentages of
p11 c0 10.2pt | analysis are labelled at the nodes. Values less than 0.95 pp and
p11 c0 10.2pt | less than 70 % bs are not shown. Branches with values more
p11 c0 10.2pt | than 1 pp and 95 % bs are thickened. Newly obtained sequences
p11 c0 10.2pt | were deposited in GenBank.
p11 c0 12.2pt | **Morphological** **analysis**
p11 c0 10.2pt | Macroscopic characters were studied on the agar media Czapek
p11 c0 10.2pt | Yeast Autolysate agar (CYA), CYA supplemented with 5 % NaCl
p11 c0 10.2pt | (CYAS), yeast extract sucrose agar (YES), creatine sucrose agar
p11 c0 10.2pt | (CREA), dichloran 18 % glycerol agar (DG18), oatmeal agar
p11 c0 10.2pt | (OA) and malt extract agar (MEA; Oxoid CM0059), trace ele-
p11 c0 10.2pt | ments (0.1 g ZnSO4$7H2O and 0.5 g CuSO4$5H2O in 100 ml
p11 c0 10.2pt | distilled water) were added to all media to obtain stable pigment
p11 c0 10.2pt | production and consistent conidial colours (Samson_ et al._ 2010).
p11 c0 10.2pt | The isolates were inoculated at three points on 90 mm plates and
p11 c0 10.2pt | incubated for 7 d at 25 °C in darkness. In addition, CYA plates
p11 c0 10.2pt | were incubated at 37 and 40 °C (CYA 37 °C and CYA 40 °C,
p11 c0 10.2pt | respectively), while additional MEA plates were incubated at
p11 c0 10.2pt | 37 °C (MEA 37 °C). After 7 d of incubation, colony diameters
p11 c0 10.2pt | were recorded. Colony texture, degree of sporulation, obverse

p11 c1 10.2pt | and are not included here.
p11 c1 12.2pt | **Cryo** **Scanning** **Electron** **Microscopy** **(cryoSEM)**
p11 c1 10.2pt | Mature ascomata were harvested from 30–50 day old cultures on
p11 c1 10.2pt | OA. Ascomata were crushed and ascospores were picked using a
p11 c1 10.2pt | dissecting needle and carefully transferred into distilled deionized
p11 c1 10.2pt | water. A drop (5 μl) of this suspension was transferred to a poly-
p11 c1 10.2pt | carbonate membrane (1.0 Micron, 47 mm, GE Water and Process
p11 c1 10.2pt | Technologies, Trevose, PA, USA). Polycarbonate membranes
p11 c1 10.2pt | were placed on filter paper circles (0.7 mm, Schleicher & Schuell)

=== page 12, left column ===
p12 c0 10.2pt | to ensure that fluid was quickly absorbed through the pores of the
p12 c0 10.2pt | membranes. The quick removal of fluid resulted in an equal dis-
p12 c0 10.2pt | tribution of ascospores and also more ascospores that could be
p12 c0 10.2pt | viewed from the equatorial side as compared with passive evap-
p12 c0 10.2pt | oration of a droplet. The polycarbonate membranes with asco-
p12 c0 10.2pt | spore depositions were carefully cut out with a surgical knife and
p12 c0 10.2pt | transferred to an aluminium stub. After drying at room temperature
p12 c0 10.2pt | for one wk, the stubs were sputter-coated with gold three times for
p12 c0 10.2pt | 30 s in a JEOL JFC-1300 Auto-fine coater and then viewed using a
p12 c0 10.2pt | JEOL 5600LV scanning electron microscope (JEOL, Tokyo,
p12 c0 10.2pt | Japan). Electron micrographs were acquired with the F4 scan at an
p12 c0 10.2pt | acceleration voltage of 10 kV.
p12 c0 12.2pt | **Extrolite** **analysis**
p12 c0 10.2pt | Representatives of 48 section_ Nidulantes_ species were analysed
p12 c0 10.2pt | for extrolite production using the method originally described by
p12 c0 10.2pt | Frisvad & Thrane (1987, 1993) and modified by Smedsgaard
p12 c0 10.2pt | (1997), and using the UHPLC-DAD method described in
p12 c0 10.2pt | Kildgaard _et_ _al._ (2014) and Klitgaard _et_ _al._ (2014). Strains were
p12 c0 10.2pt | inoculated and incubated on CYA and YES agar for 7 d at 25 °C
p12 c0 10.2pt | in darkness and subsequently three plugs were extracted as
p12 c0 10.2pt | described by Smedsgaard (1997). Species in clade_ Versicolores_
p12 c0 10.2pt | (= section_ Versicolores_) and currently described_ A. croceus_ and
p12 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _askiburgiensis_ (Hubka _et_ _al._ 2016) are not included.
p12 c0 12.2pt | **RESULTS**
p12 c0 12.2pt | **Phylogeny**
p12 c0 10.2pt | The phylogenetic relationships among _Aspergillus_ subgenus
p12 c0 10.2pt | _Nidulantes_ species were studied using concatenated sequence
p12 c0 10.2pt | data of four loci: ITS,_ BenA_,_ CaM_ and_ RPB2_. In total, 130 ex-type
p12 c0 10.2pt | strains were included in the analysis and the total length of the
p12 c0 10.2pt | aligned data set was 2483 characters, containing 498, 527, 537
p12 c0 10.2pt | and 921 bp for ITS, _BenA_, _CaM_ and _RPB2_ respectively. For
p12 c0 10.2pt | Bayesian analyses, GTR+G model was used for ITS,_ BenA_,_ CaM_
p12 c0 10.2pt | and_ RPB2_. Fig. 1 shows the results of the analysis and reveals
p12 c0 10.2pt | the presence of nine lineages in subgenus _Nidulantes_. These
p12 c0 10.2pt | lineages are treated here as sections, namely_ Aenei_,_ Nidulantes_,
p12 c0 10.2pt | _Usti_, _Raperi_, _Silvati_, _Bispori_, _Ochraceorosei_, _Sparsi_ and the
p12 c0 10.2pt | newly introduced section_ Cavernicolus_. The members of sections
p12 c0 10.2pt | _Nidulantes_ and _Versicolores_ form a well-supported group (1 pp,
p12 c0 10.2pt | 100 % ML), which is in agreement with previous studies
p12 c0 10.2pt | (Peterson 2008, Peterson _et_ _al._ 2008). On the basis of the
p12 c0 10.2pt | phylogenetic analysis we follow Hubka_ et al._ (2016) and include
p12 c0 10.2pt | _Versicolores_ within section_ Nidulantes_. Based on our results, 65
p12 c0 10.2pt | species are well resolved in section _Nidulantes_. Section _Cav-_
p12 c0 10.2pt | _ernicolus_ (1 pp, 85 % ML) contains five species previously
p12 c0 10.2pt | assigned to section _Usti_, namely_ A. californicus_,_ A._ _cavernicola_,
p12 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _egyptiacus_, _A._ _kassunensis_ and _A._ _subsessilis_. Most of spe-
p12 c0 10.2pt | cies in this section produce short conidiophores, except
p12 c0 10.2pt | _A. californicus,_ _which_ produces long, light brown conidiophores,
p12 c0 10.2pt | resembling typical section _Usti_ species (Samson _et_ _al._ 2011).
p12 c0 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ _funiculosus_ included in section _Sparsi_ by Peterson
p12 c0 10.2pt | (2008), clusters with _A._ _ochraceorosues_ and _A._ _rambellii_ with
p12 c0 10.2pt | poor bootstrap and Bayesian statistics.
p12 c0 10.2pt | To define relationships within section _Nidulantes_, an aligned
p12 c0 10.2pt | concatenated data set with a total length of 2,400 characters (ITS
p12 c0 10.2pt | 533; _BenA_ 472; _CaM_ 505; _RPB2_ 890 bp) was analysed. For

=== page 12, right column ===
p12 c1 10.2pt | Bayesian analysis, GTR+G was used for _BenA_, _CaM_ and ITS
p12 c1 10.2pt | and K2P+G for _RPB2_. Members of section _Nidulantes_ are
p12 c1 10.2pt | resolved into seven well supported clades (Fig. 2). The
p12 c1 10.2pt | _A. nidulans_ clade contains 23 species including the type species
p12 c1 10.2pt | of section _Nidulantes-A._ _nidulans._ _Aspergillus_ _dentatus_ is
p12 c1 10.2pt | phylogenetically identical with _A._ _nidulans_ and therefore
p12 c1 10.2pt | considered a synonym. Similarly, four species (_A._ _parvathecius_,
p12 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _nidulans_ var. _acristatus_, _A._ _fl__oriformis_ and _A._ _miyajii_) are
p12 c1 10.2pt | synonymised with _A._ _quadrilineatus_. _Aspergillus_ _sublatus_ and
p12 c1 10.2pt | _A. montenegroi_ are synonymised with_ A. latus_;_ A. rugulosus_ var.
p12 c1 10.2pt | _lazulina_ and _A._ _cleistominutus_ are synonymised with
p12 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _rugulosus_; _A._ _similis_ is synonymised with _A._ _violaceus_. The
p12 c1 10.2pt | relation between the clades_ A. aurantiobrunneus_,_ A. spelunceus_
p12 c1 10.2pt | and _A._ _versicolor_ are uncertain, _A._ _aurantiobrunneus_ clade
p12 c1 10.2pt | clusters outside clades _A._ _spelunceus_ and _A._ _versicolor_ in the
p12 c1 10.2pt | subgenus phylogeny (Fig. 1), while it clusters with_ A. spelunceus_
p12 c1 10.2pt | clade in the section phylogeny (Fig. 2), both of the phylograms do
p12 c1 10.2pt | not have bootstrap and Bayesian statistics. The_ A. stellatus_ clade
p12 c1 10.2pt | contains species with either stellate or appendaged ascospores.
p12 c1 10.2pt | _Aspergillus chinensis_ is considered a synonym of_ A.__ fi__lifer_ based
p12 c1 10.2pt | on phylogenetic and morphological characters as suggested by
p12 c1 10.2pt | Matsuzawa _et_ _al._ (2012) and Hubka _et_ _al._ (2016).
p12 c1 12.2pt | **Morphology**
p12 c1 10.2pt | Morphological characters of _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ are
p12 c1 10.2pt | summarised in Tables 2–4. Ascospores can be globose, sub-
p12 c1 10.2pt | globose, stellate or appendaged (Figs 3–6). The ornamentation on
p12 c1 10.2pt | the ascospore convex is informative for species identification. For
p12 c1 10.2pt | example in the_ A. nidulans_ clade, the ascospore ornamentation can
p12 c1 10.2pt | be irregularly wrinkled (_A. corrugatus_), finely pitted (_A. foveolatus_),
p12 c1 10.2pt | rugulose (_A._ _rugulosus_) or echinulate (_A._ _spinulosporus_). Asco-
p12 c1 10.2pt | spore crests are two in number in most species, four crests are only
p12 c1 10.2pt | observed in_ A. quadrilineatus_. Ascospore colour is also taxonom-
p12 c1 10.2pt | ically informative, for example the violet ascospores can easily
p12 c1 10.2pt | differentiate _A._ _violaceus_ from other section _Nidulantes_ species
p12 c1 10.2pt | (Fig. 5). In_ A._ _aurantiopurpureus_, orange ascospores can turn to
p12 c1 10.2pt | violet in older cultures (Fig. 4), which is firstly observed in section
p12 c1 10.2pt | _Nidulantes_. However, ascospore colour can be variable in some
p12 c1 10.2pt | species. Peintner & Rainer (1999) reported an isolate of_ A. nidulans_
p12 c1 10.2pt | (CBS 100522) with blue ascospores; another example is
p12 c1 10.2pt | _A. miraensis_, which was originally described with violet ascospores
p12 c1 10.2pt | (Zhang_ etal._ 2013), but shows orangetoreddish brown ascospores
p12 c1 10.2pt | in our study. Ascomata, when present, are mostly 200–600 μm, but
p12 c1 10.2pt | it may be highly variable depending on the media, and in some
p12 c1 10.2pt | species like _A._ _quadrilineatus_ and _A._ _violaceus_, variable size of
p12 c1 10.2pt | ascomata were observed in different strains even under same
p12 c1 10.2pt | cultivation condition. Thus the ascoma size is not recommended as
p12 c1 10.2pt | a distinguishing feature.
p12 c1 10.2pt | In general, species in_ Aspergillus_ section_ Nidulantes_ produce
p12 c1 10.2pt | more or less brown-pigmented conidiophores, typically smooth but
p12 c1 10.2pt | occasionally showing surface protuberances. Vesicles are usually
p12 c1 10.2pt | globose, subglobose or subclavate, narrower than 30 μm. Conidia
p12 c1 10.2pt | are typically globose and echinulate, green in mass, in some cases
p12 c1 10.2pt | (_A. asperescens_ and_ A. varians_) conidia are ellipsoidal (Figs 7, 8).
p12 c1 10.2pt | For non-ascosporic species, size and shape of conidiophores and
p12 c1 10.2pt | conidia are taxonomically informative (Table 2).
p12 c1 10.2pt | Macromorphology including temperature growth profile, pro-
p12 c1 10.2pt | duction of cleistothecia, mycelium colour, sporulation, soluble
p12 c1 10.2pt | pigments, and exudate is also important distinguishing character.
p12 c1 10.2pt | Species within the_ A. nidulans_ clade grow optimally at 37°C but

=== page 14, left column ===
p14 c0 10.2pt | do not grow at 50 °C (Table 5, Figs 9, 10), while species in the
p14 c0 10.2pt | other six clades cannot grow at 40 °C, some species such as
p14 c0 10.2pt | _A. asperescens_,_ A. aureolatus_,_ A. pluriseminatus_,_ A. spelunceus_
p14 c0 10.2pt | and _A._ _varians_ cannot grow at 36 °C (Table 5, Figs 11, 12).
p14 c0 12.2pt | **Extrolites**
p14 c0 10.2pt | Fourty eight species were analysed for extrolites and produced
p14 c0 10.2pt | several shared or unique small molecule extrolites and often had
p14 c0 10.2pt | species specific profiles (mentioned after each species descrip-
p14 c0 10.2pt | tion). An overview of reported extrolites from section _Nidulantes_
p14 c0 10.2pt | species is shown in Table 6. Sterigmatocystins, shamixanthones,
p14 c0 10.2pt | and violaceols are common to many species and are also found in
p14 c0 10.2pt | some species from sections _Usti_ and _Aenei_ (Houbraken _et_ _al._
p14 c0 10.2pt | 2007, Varga _et_ _al._ 2010a, Samson _et_ _al._ 2011). The shamix-
p14 c0 10.2pt | anthones are produced by 19 species in section_ Nidulantes_. The
p14 c0 10.2pt | ascospore / Hülle cell-associate metabolite asperthecin is pro-
p14 c0 10.2pt | duced by 20 species in the section. The desertorin polyketides are
p14 c0 10.2pt | produced by 13 species, while violaceol polyketidesare found in 19
p14 c0 10.2pt | species. The falconensins and falconensons are produced by the
p14 c0 10.2pt | closely related species _A._ _aurantiopurpureus,_ _A._ _falconensis,_ _A._
p14 c0 10.2pt | _fruticulosus,_ _A._ _navahoensis_ and _A._ _revurvatus_. Emericellin is
p14 c0 10.2pt | found in 18 species, asperugin polyketides is produced by 14
p14 c0 10.2pt | species and the shikimic derived emerins are formed by two

=== page 14, right column ===
p14 c1 10.2pt | species. The dithiodiketopiparazine mycotoxin emestrin is pro-
p14 c1 10.2pt | duced by six closely related species:_ A. foveolatus, A. jaipurensis,_
p14 c1 10.2pt | _A. quadrilineatus, A. rugulosus, A. striatus_ and_ A. violaceus_. The
p14 c1 10.2pt | important antibiotic echinocandin and mulundocandin producers
p14 c1 10.2pt | include _A._ _mulundensis,_ _A._ _navahoensis,_ _A._ _pachycristatus,_ _A._
p14 c1 10.2pt | _quadrilineatus_, and_ A. rugulosus_ (Bills_ et al._ 2016, de la Cruz_ et al._
p14 c1 10.2pt | 2012). One of the originally reported producers was first identified
p14 c1 10.2pt | as_ A. spinulosporus_ (as_ Emericella echinulata_), but was later re-
p14 c1 10.2pt | identified as_ A. rugulosus_ (_Emericella rugulosa_) (Dreyfuss 1986).
p14 c1 10.2pt | The mycotoxin sterigmatocystin has not only been found in
p14 c1 10.2pt | most species of sections_ Nidulantes_, _Aenei_ and_ Usti_, but also in
p14 c1 10.2pt | species of section_ Ochraceorosei_ (Table 7) (Frisvad 1985, Horie &
p14 c1 10.2pt | Yamazaki 1985, Rank_ et al._ 2011, Jurjevic_ et al._ 2013). In_ Asper-_
p14 c1 10.2pt | _gillus_ section _Nidulantes_ 35 species could produce ster-
p14 c1 10.2pt | igmatocystin, four additional species (_A. multicolor, A. purpureus,_
p14 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _stellatus_ and _A._ _violaceus_) have been reported to produce
p14 c1 10.2pt | sterigmatocystin, but this could not be confirmed here, and two
p14 c1 10.2pt | species remains to be examined for production of sterigmatocystin
p14 c1 10.2pt | (_A. omanensis_ and_ A. sulphureoviridis_) (Table 7). Aflatoxin B1 is
p14 c1 10.2pt | produced by four species:_ A. astellatus, A. miraensis, A. olivicola_,
p14 c1 10.2pt | and_ A. venezuelensis_. This is the first report of aflatoxin production
p14 c1 10.2pt | by _A._ _miraensis_. Other mycotoxins are also produced by a few
p14 c1 10.2pt | species in section _Nidulantes_, such as verruculogen and fumi-
p14 c1 10.2pt | tremorgins in _A._ _caespitosus_ and asteltoxin produced by

=== page 17, left column ===
p17 c0 10.2pt | _A. olivicola_,_ A. qinqixianii_,_ A. stellatus_ and_ A.__ fi__lifer_. Thus the most
p17 c0 10.2pt | important mycotoxins in _Aspergillus_ section _Nidulantes_ are afla-
p17 c0 10.2pt | toxins, sterigmatocystin, emestrin, fumitremorgins, asteltoxins, and
p17 c0 10.2pt | paxillin while other extrolites are useful drugs or drug lead candi-
p17 c0 10.2pt | dates such as echinocandins, mulundocandins, calbistrins, vari-
p17 c0 10.2pt | triols, variecolins and terrein, and some can be regarded as both
p17 c0 10.2pt | mycotoxins and drug lead candidates, for example vir-
p17 c0 10.2pt | idicatumtoxin. It is interesting to note that many of these com-
p17 c0 10.2pt | pounds are also produced by other _Aspergillus_ species in
p17 c0 10.2pt | phylogenetically different subgenera, showing that species in
p17 c0 10.2pt | section_ Nidulantes_ are quite closely related to these other species
p17 c0 10.2pt | in many features (Frisvad & Larsen 2015).
p17 c0 12.2pt | **DISCUSSION**
p17 c0 12.2pt | **Sectional** **classi****fi****cation** **in** **subgenus** **Nidulantes**
p17 c0 10.2pt | Based on a multigene phylogeny, nine sections are proposed
p17 c0 10.2pt | within subgenus _Nidulantes_. Eight of them were introduced in
p17 c0 10.2pt | previous studies (Peterson 2008, Peterson _et_ _al._ 2008, Varga
p17 c0 10.2pt | _et_ _al._ 2010a, b). Five species previously assigned in section
p17 c0 10.2pt | _Usti_, namely _A._ _californicus_, _A._ _cavernicola_, _A._ _egyptiacus_,
p17 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _kassunensis_ and _A._ _subsessilis_ form a monophyletic clade
p17 c0 10.2pt | outside section_ Usti_. The bootstrap support of this distinct clade
p17 c0 10.2pt | is low (Hubka _et_ _al._ 2016), but the species within this clade do

=== page 17, right column ===
p17 c1 10.2pt | share some phenotypic characters, most of them (except
p17 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _californicus_) produce short conidiophores, which is not com-
p17 c1 10.2pt | mon in section _Usti_. Based on these observations, we propose
p17 c1 10.2pt | _Aspergillus_ section_ Cavernicolus_ to accommodate these species
p17 c1 10.2pt | within subgenus _Nidulantes_. Section _Aenei_ was included in
p17 c1 10.2pt | section _Nidulantes_ as _Aspergillus_ _aeneus_ clade (Hubka _et_ _al._
p17 c1 10.2pt | 2016). However in our study, section _Aenei_ locates outside
p17 c1 10.2pt | section _Nidulantes_ with full support, which agrees with Varga
p17 c1 10.2pt | _et_ _al._ (2010a). Phenotypically the homothallic species in sec-
p17 c1 10.2pt | tion _Aenei_ (_A._ _discophorus_, _A._ _bicolor_, _A._ _spectabilis_ and
p17 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _foeniculicola_) produce similar ascospores with _A._ _nidulans_
p17 c1 10.2pt | clade, but none of them is able to grow at 40 °C. Based on these
p17 c1 10.2pt | observations, section _Aenei_ is kept to accommodate these
p17 c1 10.2pt | species. The placement of _A._ _funiculosus_ in a certain group is
p17 c1 10.2pt | doubtful, Raper & Fennell (1965) accepted_ A. funiculosus_ as the
p17 c1 10.2pt | only uniseriate species in section _Sparsi_ (_Aspergillus_ _sparsus_
p17 c1 10.2pt | group), our phylogenetic results show that_ A. funiculosus_ is more
p17 c1 10.2pt | closely to _A._ _ochraceoroseus_, however is not supported by
p17 c1 10.2pt | bootstrap and Bayesian statistics as shown by Hubka _et_ _al._
p17 c1 10.2pt | (2016), the belonging of this species needs further study.
p17 c1 12.2pt | **Clade** **classi****fi****cation** **in** **section** **Nidulantes**
p17 c1 10.2pt | Matsuzawa _et_ _al._ (2012) performed the first multilocus analysis
p17 c1 10.2pt | based on _BenA_, _CaM_ and _actin_ in the genus _Emericella_, eight
p17 c1 10.2pt | clades were introduced for 37 species, clades I, II, III, IV, V and VI

=== page 18, left column ===
p18 c0 10.2pt | are equal with our_ A. nidulans_ clade, clades VII and VIII are equal
p18 c0 10.2pt | with our_ A. stellatus_ clade, anamorphic species were not included
p18 c0 10.2pt | in their analysis. Hubka _et_ _al._ (2016) constructed a phylogenetic
p18 c0 10.2pt | analysis for section _Nidulantes_, six statistically supported clades
p18 c0 10.2pt | were designated, namely clades _A._ _aeneus_, _A._ _spelunceus_,
p18 c0 10.2pt | _A. versicolor_,_ A. stellatus_,_ A. nidulans_ and_ A. unguis_, five of them
p18 c0 10.2pt | are confirmed in our study, while _Aspergillus_ _aeneus_ clade is
p18 c0 10.2pt | treated as section _Aenei_ as discussed above. Besides these
p18 c0 10.2pt | clades, additional two clades are introduced here, namely clades
p18 c0 10.2pt | _A._ _aurantiobrunneus_ and _A._ _multicolor_. The _A._ _aurantiobrunneus_

=== page 18, right column ===
p18 c1 10.2pt | clade contains _A._ _aurantiobrunneus_ and _A._ _purpureus_. Hubka
p18 c1 10.2pt | _et_ _al._ (2016) included _A._ _aurantiobrunneus_ in _A._ _spelunceus_
p18 c1 10.2pt | clade although the grouping was not well supported (100/80/1 ML/
p18 c1 10.2pt | MP/PP),_ A. purpureus_ was not included in their study. In our study,
p18 c1 10.2pt | these two species form a full supported branch (1 pp, 100 % ML),
p18 c1 10.2pt | morphologically they all produce globose and subglobose asco-
p18 c1 10.2pt | spores and grow restrictedly on all tested media. In contrast all
p18 c1 10.2pt | species in_ A. spelunceus_ clade are anamorphic, and grow faster.
p18 c1 10.2pt | Another newly introduced clade is_ A. multicolor_ clade, this clade
p18 c1 10.2pt | contains_ A. multicolor_,_ A. mulundensis_ and_ A. pluriseminatus_, and

=== page 19, right column ===
p19 c1 10.2pt | is close related with _A._ _nidulans_ clade. No species in this clade
p19 c1 10.2pt | grow at 40 °C,_ A. pluriseminatus_, the only homothallic species in
p19 c1 10.2pt | this clade produces stellate ascospores, which show more simi-
p19 c1 10.2pt | larity with species in_ A. stellatus_ clade.
p19 c1 12.2pt | **Aspergillus** **section** **Nidulantes**
p19 c1 10.2pt | Anamorph present or absent, if present conidiophores more or
p19 c1 10.2pt | less brown-pigmented, typically smooth but occasionally showing
p19 c1 10.2pt | protuberances, usually sinuous; vesicles usually globose, sub-
p19 c1 10.2pt | globose or subclavate, biseriate, metulae and phialides usually
p19 c1 10.2pt | about equal in length. Conidia globose to subglobose, ovate to
p19 c1 10.2pt | ellipsoidal, echinulate or finely rough, less commonly smooth.
p19 c1 10.2pt | Ascomata produced in most species, but lacking in others;
p19 c1 10.2pt | emericella-like, cleistothecial, superficial, solitary or clustered,
p19 c1 10.2pt | globose to subglobose, non-ostiolate, reddish brown, violet, dark
p19 c1 10.2pt | brown or blackish, typically surrounded by a heavy to discon-
p19 c1 10.2pt | tinuous layer of Hülle cells; Hülle cells hyaline, pale brown, or-
p19 c1 10.2pt | ange brown or pink, globose, subglobose, pyriform or ovoid. Asci
p19 c1 10.2pt | 8 spored, globose to subglobose or stellate, evanescent. Asco-
p19 c1 10.2pt | spores one-celled, orange, purplish, violet, reddish brown or
p19 c1 10.2pt | brown, globose to subglobose or stellate, usually with equatorial
p19 c1 10.2pt | crests, smooth or with different patterns of ornamentation, entire,
p19 c1 10.2pt | dentate, defective or with filiform appendages.
p19 c1 10.2pt | _Typus_: _Aspergillus_ _nidulans_ (Eidam) G. Winter, Rabenh. Krypt.-
p19 c1 10.2pt | Fl., ed. 2, 1: 62. 1884
p19 c1 12.2pt | **Aspergillus** **nidulans** **clade**
p19 c1 10.2pt | _Description_: Conidiophores (if present) typically smooth but oc-
p19 c1 10.2pt | casionally showing surface protuberances, hyaline to yellowish
p19 c1 10.2pt | brown; vesicles globose to subclavate, fertile over the upper half
p19 c1 10.2pt | to two thirds; Conidia echinulate, globose to subglobose. Asco-
p19 c1 10.2pt | mata (if present), cleistothecial, superficial, reddish brown, violet
p19 c1 10.2pt | or dark brown, globose to subglobose, surrounded by numerous
p19 c1 10.2pt | Hülle cells; Hülle cells hyaline to pale brown, globose, sub-
p19 c1 10.2pt | globose pyriform or ovoid. Asci 8 spored, globose to subglobose.
p19 c1 10.2pt | Ascospores orange, reddish brown, brown or violet, in surface
p19 c1 10.2pt | view globose to subglobose, spore bodies smooth or with
p19 c1 10.2pt | verrucose, echinulate, reticulate or pitted ornamentation. Asco-
p19 c1 10.2pt | spore crests entire, dentate, defective or with irregular protu-
p19 c1 10.2pt | berance, inconspicuous in some species, mostly two in number,
p19 c1 10.2pt | four crests are present in _A._ _quadrilineatus._ Most species grow
p19 c1 10.2pt | optimally around 37 °C, do not grow at or above 50 °C,
p19 c1 10.2pt | _A. botswanensis_,_ A. fruticulosus_,_ A. latilabiatus_ and_ A. recurvatus_
p19 c1 10.2pt | do not grow at 45 °C (Table 5). Twenty-three species are

p38 c1 10.2pt | **Aspergillus** **aurantiobrunneus** (G.A. Atkins, Hindson & A.B.
p38 c1 10.2pt | Russell) Raper & Fennell, Gen. _Aspergillus_: 511. 1965.

p38 c1 12.2pt | **Phylogenetic** **species** **recognition**
p38 c1 10.2pt | Based on a concatenated sequence analysis, 65 species are well
p38 c1 10.2pt | resolved in section_ Nidulantes_. ITS, the recommended official DNA
p38 c1 10.2pt | barcode for fungi (Schoch_ et al._ 2012), performs well in recognizing
p38 c1 10.2pt | species in clades _A._ _unguis_, _A._ _spelunceus_ and _A._ _multicolor_.
p38 c1 10.2pt | However it is not variable sufficiently for recognizing species in
p38 c1 10.2pt | other clades. _BenA_, _CaM_ and _RPB2_ can identify 63 species
p38 c1 10.2pt | respectively, with_ A. quadrilineatus_ sharing identical_ BenA_ with_ A._
p38 c1 10.2pt | _latus_, _A._ _qinqixianii_ and _A._ _fi__lifer_ sharing identical _CaM_ and
p38 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _rugulosus_ and _A._ _pachycristatus_ sharing identical _RPB2_ se-
p38 c1 10.2pt | quences. Matsuzawa _et_ _al._ (2012) stated that _A._ _nidulans_ (=
p38 c1 10.2pt | _E. nidulans_),_ A. dentatus_ (=_ E. dentata_),_ A. latus_ (=_ E. nidulans_ var.
p38 c1 10.2pt | _lata_),_A. sublatus_ (=_ E. sublata_),_ A. montenegroi_ (=_ E. montenegroi_),
p38 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _acristatus_ (= _E._ _acristata_), _A._ _miyajii_ (= _E._ _miyajii_),
p38 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _quadrilineatus_ (= _E._ _quadrilineata_) and _A._ _parvathecius_ (=
p38 c1 10.2pt | _E._ _parvathecia_) were undistinguishable by phylogenetic analysis
p38 c1 10.2pt | alone. These are confirmed in this study, to follow the genealogical
p38 c1 10.2pt | concordance phylogenetic species recognition concept (GCPSR),
p38 c1 10.2pt | several species are considered as synonyms here:_ A. dentatus_ is
p38 c1 10.2pt | synonymised with_ A. nidulans_;_ A. sublatus_ and_ A. montenegroi_ are
p38 c1 10.2pt | synonymised with _A._ _latus_; _A._ _acristatus_, _A._ _miyajii_ and
p38 c1 10.2pt | _A._ _parvathecius_ are synonymised with _A._ _quadrilineatus_ as did
p38 c1 10.2pt | Hubka _et_ _al._ 2016. Overall species in _A._ _nidulans_ clade
p38 c1 10.2pt | are phylogenetically similar, both phylogenetic and morphological
p38 c1 10.2pt | data are important to define the species boundary. Speices in other
p38 c1 10.2pt | six clades are more divergent phylogenetically, the only exceptions
p38 c1 10.2pt | are_ A. qinqixianii_ and_ A.__ fi__lifer_, they share identical_ CaM_ sequences,
p38 c1 10.2pt | only small differences are present in_ BenA_ (99.7 % similarity, 344/
p38 c1 10.2pt | 345 bp), _actin_ (98.9 % similarity, 366/370 bp) (Matsuzawa _et_ _al._
p38 c1 10.2pt | 2012, Hubka_ et al._ 2016) and_ RPB2_ (99.7 % similarity, 912/914 bp).
p38 c1 12.2pt | **Morphological** **species** **recognition**
p38 c1 10.2pt | For homothallic species in section _Nidulantes_, the ascospore
p38 c1 10.2pt | shape, ornamentation, color and size are of particular impor-
p38 c1 10.2pt | tance for differentiating species (Thom & Raper 1939,
p38 c1 10.2pt | Christensen & Raper 1978, Horie 1980, Christensen & States
p38 c1 10.2pt | 1982, Ismail _et_ _al._ 1995, Zalar _et_ _al._ 2008, Matsuzawa _et_ _al._
p38 c1 10.2pt | 2012, Guarro _et_ _al._ 2012). In _A._ _nidulans_ clade, most species
p38 c1 10.2pt | have unique ascospore morphology (Figs 3–5). However in
p38 c1 10.2pt | some species, ascospore morphology shows a range of di-
p38 c1 10.2pt | versity. For example most of _A._ _nidulans_ strains have entire

=== page 41, left column ===
p41 c0 10.2pt | crests, but atypical dentate crests are presented in one strain
p41 c0 10.2pt | (CBS 114.63), similarly in _A._ _quadrilineatus_, the crests in as-
p41 c0 10.2pt | cospores can be entire, defective or with irregular protuber-
p41 c0 10.2pt | ance. In_ A. stellatus_ clade, species with stellate ascospores are
p41 c0 10.2pt | morphologically very similar, molecular identification is recom-
p41 c0 10.2pt | mended for distinguishing these species. Anamorphic struc-
p41 c0 10.2pt | tures are also important for species recognition, especially for
p41 c0 10.2pt | anamorphic species. But the anamorphic structures can be
p41 c0 10.2pt | affected by media and incubation conditions, here we follow the
p41 c0 10.2pt | standardized method for laboratories working with _Aspergillus_
p41 c0 10.2pt | (Samson _et_ _al._ 2014), MEA is recommended for anamorphic
p41 c0 10.2pt | description and OA is recommended for teleomorphic
p41 c0 10.2pt | description in section _Nidulantes_.
